Core functions top-level:
```
import numpy as np, time, math

def fft_size(n):
    m=2*n-1
    s=1
    while s<m: s<<=1
    return s
```

Mirror descent update:
```
def update_multiplicative(a, grad, eta, temp=1.0):
    # grad can be any scale; we normalize by grad statistic
    # center? We'll use centered to avoid drift: grad_c = grad - np.mean(grad)
    # But for mirror on simplex, constant irrelevant; center optional.
    grad_c = grad - np.mean(grad)
    scale = np.mean(np.abs(grad_c))
    if scale==0: return a
    step = np.exp(-eta * grad_c / scale) # eta maybe [0.01..1]
    a_new = a * step
    s = a_new.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return a
    return a_new * n / s
```
If grad_c has large negative/positive, step can overflow. Clip `grad_c/scale` to [-8,8] before exp. Or use `np.exp(np.clip(...,-10,10))`.

4. `mode='target'`; weights = c - target (target maybe current max*0.9? or mean).

Optimization loop details:

We can write a function `optimize(a0, budget, seed, mode_sequence)` that runs time budget, returns best a, score.

Within `optimize`:
- Set N based on length n.
- `a = positive normalize` to sum n.
- `best_a`, `best_score` computed exact via FFT.
- Maintain state for Adam? If using mirror line search no state.
- For each phase (e.g., 5 seconds blocks):
   - Determine gradient mode/params according to progress or random.
   - Compute c, fa maybe.
   - For several candidate weights (e.g., weight mixes) and several step sizes, compute updated a and score, pick best.
   - If improvement, update best; else try perturbation/restart.

This is a stochastic/parallel free search. Need efficient.

Let's design `one_step(a, N, mode, params, seed)` returning best candidate among a batch.

Multiplicative update:
```
def multiplicative_step(a, grad, eta, max_log=8):
    # grad length n, can any; normalize
    mean = np.mean(grad)
    g = grad - mean
    scale = np.mean(np.abs(g))
    if scale < 1e-12: return a.copy()
    z = np.clip(-eta * g / scale, -max_log, max_log)
    a_new = a * np.exp(z)
    s = a_new.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not finite: return a.copy()
    a_new *= (n / s)
    # optional clip upper 1000?
    if a_new.max() > 1000: a_new = np.minimum(a_new,1000); a_new *= n / a_new.sum()
    return a_new
```
But subtracting mean unweighted changes gradient constant. If many small a, mean may be dominated by few large gradients? Since n equal. Good.

For variance gradient with negative weights, scale may be large. eta maybe 0.01-0.5.

Line search etas: e.g., [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4]. Use best. Since objective scale invariant and update normalized, eta dimensionless.

- `top_soft`: weights = exp((c - cmax)/τ). Need τ. Adaptive:
   Compute sorted c descending maybe expensive each step? L=2n-1 ~16k; sorting 16k*10000 =160M comparisons heavy but okay? We can use percentile approximation via quickselect? Need avoid many sorts. We can estimate τ from `cmax - cmean` and `cmax - quantile`. Sort maybe every 100 steps. Simpler: Use fixed τ relative to c: For sum=n, cmax~7000. Spread maybe? Start coarse τ=500, fine τ=50. We can anneal over time: τ = τ_start * (τ_end/τ_start)^progress. But for adapt, use `τ = max(1.0, 0.1 * (cmax - c_median))`. Need median. Use `np.quantile(c, 0.5)` O(L) no sort? numpy quantile may quickselect? It might sort. But L 16k, okay maybe. Could compute every step? 16k*10000=160M operations, maybe okay in C but quantile uses introsort maybe heavy. Use `np.percentile` each 50 steps. Or compute fixed.

   Phase 0 (first 10% time): coarse, τ = 0.25 * n? cmax~3000? If sum n, uniform cmax=n=6121. τ ~500. Use `lp` p=1? Actually max_soft with large τ ~ average weights. Variance better.
   Phase 1: τ = 0.1 * n?

Maybe a combined weight: `w = alpha * softmax(c/τ) + (1-alpha) * normalized (c - mean)` with both positive? Negative allowed. For line search, can use combined gradient. This balances max and flatness.

Let's implement weight functions:
```
def weights_logsumexp(c, tau):
    m=c.max(); e=np.exp((c-m)/tau); s=e.sum(); return e/s if s>0 else np.ones_like(c)/len(c)

def weights_lift(c):
    mu=c.mean()
    w=c-mu
    # ensure sum zero; scale to max abs 1
    s=np.max(np.abs(w))
    if s>0: w = w/s
    return w

def weights_lp(c, p):
    cc=np.maximum(c,0)
    if p==1: w=cc/cc.sum()
    else: w=cc**p; w/=w.sum()
    return w
```
For negative weights, normalize by sum abs maybe.

Gradient from weights: `grad = 2 * corr(a, w, N)[:n]`. Note weights sum may not be 1; scale absorbed by eta. For variance, w sum zero; grad mean? May have constant? Centering.

Could include "target" weights: `w = c - target`, target = `cmax * target_ratio`, e.g., 0.75. This penalizes above target and pulls below. `target_ratio` maybe 0.8-1.0. Since average ~0.5cmax if flat? For optimum cmax=1.5 avg, avg=0.667cmax. target maybe 0.9cmax to reduce top and raise low. Weight scale.

Maybe use `w = np.maximum(c - target, 0)` for top only plus uniform negative? But if only top, others scale up due to normalization, may raise low. Good. Use top threshold.

Let's design `get_direction(a,c,mode,cmax,tau, target_frac)`:
- mode 0 'top': weights softmax, gradient.
- mode 1 'var': weights c-mean.
- mode 2 'lp1': weights c normalized (this is gradient of ∑c^2? Actually weights c, not normalized; okay).

Adaptive τ formula: Want top fraction f active. Let `c_t = c_max - c_{q}` where q = (1-f) quantile (e.g., 0.9 quantile). If all weights exp(-Δ/τ), effective count ≈ exp(cmax-∫?) Not exact. We can define a function `adaptive_tau(c, f)`:
```
L=len(c); m=int(L*(1-f)); if m>=L: m=L-1
vals = np.partition(c, m)[m] # L sorted asc, index m gives (1-f) quantile? For f=0.1, m=0.9L, value 90th percentile.
gap = c.max() - vals
tau = gap / (-log(f))
return max(1e-6, tau)
```
For f=0.5, log2=0.69 -> tau large.

Optimization routine for a selected init:
Parameters: `budget`, `seed`, `tau_f_top` maybe. We'll run a loop with time slices.
Pseudo:
```
def run_optimizer(a_init, budget, seed, verbose=True):
   n=len(a); N=pow2(2n-1)
   a=normalize(a,n)
   best_a=a.copy(); best_c=max_conv(a,N)
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
   rng=...
   no_improve_steps=0
   step=0
   # state for Adam? Maybe not.
   while time < deadline:
       # choose mode based on phase and random
       progress = (time-start)/budget
       # If no improve for many, try larger eta/perturb
       modes = ['soft','var','lp1','target','soft_fine']
       # choose mode via weighted random or schedule
       # compute c and max
       c, max_c = conv(a,N)
       # if max_c < best_c: update best
       # get direction(s)
       directions=[]
       if mode soft: tau=adaptive_tau(c, f_top) with f_top schedule 0.2->0.01; w=softmax; g=corr2
       if mode var: w=(c-mean)/scale; g=corr2
       if mode lp1: w=c/csum; g=corr2
       if target: w=clip(c - target,0); w/=max; g=corr2
       # Combine maybe: g = a_weight*top_g + b_weight*var_g
       # line search
       base_score=max_c
       best_step_a=None; best_step_c=base_score
       for eta in eta_candidates:
           cand = multiplicative_update(a, g, eta)
           cand_c, cand_max=conv(cand,N)
           if cand_max < best_step_c: ...
       if best_step_c < base_score*(1-1e-9): accept a=best_step_a; if best_step_c < best_c: update best; no_improve=0; else no_improve++
       else:
           no_improve++
           # try random perturbation
           cand = random_perturb(a, rng, strength)
           if score improves accept
           if no_improve > threshold:
               # bigger perturbation or revert best
               a = best_a * random noise (stronger)
               no_improve=0
       step++
   return best_a,best_c
```

Need compute correlation gradient efficiently. Function:
```
def corr_from_fft(a weights? )
def gradient_from_weights(a, w, N):
    # weights length L=2n-1; pad to N
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    r=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fa)*fw,N)[:n]
    return 2.0*r
```
But if we compute c in loop, we already have fa. For each gradient direction, need fa. We can compute fa once per step: `fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)`, c=irfft(fa*fa). For gradient with weights: `fw=rfft(w,N)`, `r=irfft(conj(fa)*fw,N)[:n]`, grad=2r. Good.

Perturbations:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.1, n=None):
   # multiplicative noise on blocks
   cand=a.copy()
   for _ in range(3): width=rng.integers(n//100,n//10); start=...; cand[start:end]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength,width))
   # global noise small
   cand*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength*0.1,n))
   return normalize(cand,n,1000)
```
Accept if improves exact max. Else if no_improve large, try stronger or reinitialize from best with noise; update a to best copy plus noise; reset.

Crossover: Maintain `best_a`; when no_improve > threshold, try `cand = sqrt(best_a * a)` normalized and maybe `arithmetic mean`, evaluate. If better apply.

LP kick implementation:
We can include a function `lp_kick(a,budget)` that tries to improve. It uses penalty multiplicative on g.
Pseudo:
```
def lp_kick(a, N, seed, max_iter=200, time_limit=5):
   n=len(a); L=2n-1
   M = max_conv(a,N)
   a_norm = normalize(a,n)
   g = a.copy()  # start
   eta_g = 0.1
   for it:
      c, M = conv(a,N) # M fixed? current max
      z, _ = cross_conv? z = a*g full length L. Compute via FFT: fa, fg; zg=irfft(fa*fg)[:L]
      lam = np.maximum(z/M - 1.0, 0.0)
      # gradient for dual: h=corr(a, lam)
      fh = rfft(lam,N); h=irfft(conj(fa)*fh,N)[:n] # =∑ a_i lam_{i+m}
      # multiplicative gradient for g: s = 1 - h / scale? h scale ~? lam sum maybe; scale = max(h)
      s = 1.0 - alpha * h / (h.mean()+eps)
      g = g * exp(clip(eta_g * s, -maxlog,maxlog)); normalize sum n; g=np.maximum(g,0)
   cand = normalize(g + eps, n)
   score; if better return g
```
Not guaranteed. Could spend little time.

Another kick: "inverse target" spectral:
Given current a, compute c. We can adjust a in frequency to make c flatter: At linearized level around current a, changing a by δ changes c by 2a*δ. We want reduce high coefficients. Solve min ||δ||^2 s.t. (a*δ)_k <= penalties. This is a linear least squares; can solve via FFT? The operator T: δ -> a*δ (convolution) is circulant if zero-padded? Actually linear convolution can be represented by N-point circulant matrix with first column a (if no wrap). We can solve regression in Fourier: minimize ||Fδ|| weighted for high c? We want δ such that Tδ at high coefficients negative, at low positive, preserving sum. This is like applying a Fourier-domain correction. We can design gradient already. But maybe a smoothed inverse filter: Choose desired δ in time via inverse FFT of something. Not.

Let's look at KKT: For an interior optimum with active set weights p, gradient `h_i=const`. Since `h_i=2∑ a_j p_{i+j}`. If we set p to weights on c near max, this is a deconvolution: find p such that a*p ≈ constant. That can be solved in Fourier: `P = C/A`? If `a*p` (cross-correlation) constant, p = constant * conv? For given a, we can find active weights that make gradient constant; then local optimum condition. Could use this to adjust a? Not.

Maybe use "oscillation" to enforce balance: At each step, choose weights p that solve `a*p = const + small`, then p are active constraints. But p nonnegative? Could compute p via inverse filter and clip. Not needed.

Let's also consider using `scipy.optimize.minimize` with our gradient in log space for final polishing. But implementing with L-BFGS-B might be heavy. Could use Numpy only.

Potential numerical stability of FFT weights with negative variance weights: `rfft` of negative array fine. `irfft` real. The gradient may have small imaginary. Use `np.real`.

Function `convolve_pair_fft(a,b,N)` maybe for `a*g`. For candidate score, `conv_self`.

Use `np.maximum(c,0.0)` to remove negative tiny; but if c has negative significant due to roundoff? a nonnegative so c should nonnegative; clip.

Now, objective exact with normalization: Let's define:
```
def normalize(a, n, cap=1000.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, cap)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 0: return np.full(n, n/n?)
    a = a * (n / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, cap)
    s2=a.sum()
    if s2>0: a *= n/s2 else uniform
    return a
```
If cap after scaling causes sum <n. Could iterate or accept. capping at 1000 rarely. If n>1000 and all equal, values ~1<1000. If one huge, cap. Fine.

```
c = self_conv(a, size)
s = a.sum()
if s<0.01: return inf
max_c = c.max()
return 2.0*n*max_c/(s*s), max_c
```
In internal after normalize s=n; use `score_by_max = 2.0*max_c/n`. But if cap changed s, use formula.

For line search accept if score smaller (using S formula) not just max; but normalize ensures same. Use score robust.

```
import numpy as np
import time

def next_pow2(m):
    s=1
    while s < m: s <<= 1
    return s

def normalize_sequence(a, n, cap=1000.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size != n:
       # resize? maybe not
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, cap)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return np.full(n, n/n? actually sum n, each 1)
    a *= (float(n) / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, cap)
    s2 = a.sum()
    if s2 <= 1e-300:
       return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a *= (float(n) / s2)
    # If cap binding, sum may be <n; do another simple loop without cap? 
    # We can not renormalize after second clip to avoid exceeding cap; but sum less than n changes objective. Better allow exceeding? Evaluator caps 1000, output must <=1000. If after second still >1000, clip and accept lower sum. But internal score assume n. We can set internal normalization target not exactly n but sum actual.
    return a
```

Need handle cap. Since cap 1000, and n maybe 8192, if any a>1000, clipping reduces sum. We can normalize without cap to sum n, then if max>1000, we can cap and accept sum <n; score formula using actual sum. But target sum? The evaluator uses actual sum after cap. Internal score should match. So in internal, after clipping to cap, compute S=a.sum(); do not force sum n after final cap. For optimization, we want sum ideally n as scorescale; if cap binding, use actual S. To avoid cap, mark values not too high. We can normalize without cap; if max>cap, scale down to cap then sum <n. This may worsen. But rare. Let's define `normalize_positive(a, target_sum, cap=1000.0)`:
```
a=clip finite >=0
s=a.sum()
if s<=0: return ones
a *= target_sum/s
if a.max() > cap:
   a = np.minimum(a, cap)
   # optionally renormalize if max still? no
   # but then sum may < target. To restore sum without exceeding cap, distribute deficit to elements below cap:
   deficit = target_sum - a.sum()
   if deficit >0:
      mask = a < cap
      if mask.sum()>0:
         per = deficit / mask.sum()
         a[mask] = np.minimum(a[mask]+per, cap)
         # iterative redistribution if still deficit
         for _ in range(5):
            deficit = target_sum - a.sum()
            if abs(deficit) < 1e-9: break
            mask = a < cap
            if mask.sum()==0: break
            a[mask] = np.minimum(a[mask]+deficit/mask.sum(), cap)
   # if deficit negative (sum>target) scale down
   s=a.sum(); if s>target: a *= target/s (will reduce max below cap okay)
return a
```
This maintains sum near target without exceeding cap. Good.

But if target_sum=n=8192 and cap=1000, all uniform 1. If one element 2000, cap to 1000, redistribute deficit to others, likely okay. Implement top-level `distribute_deficit`.

`adaptive_tau(c, f_top)`:
```
L=c.size
idx = int(round(L * (1.0 - f_top)))
idx = min(max(idx,1), L-1)
q = np.partition(c, idx)[idx]
gap = c.max() - q
if gap <= 0: gap = max(1e-6, c.max()*1e-6)
tau = gap / max(1.0, -math.log(max(f_top,1e-9)))
return tau
```

```
def fourier_flat_init(n, N, seed, target_ratio=1.0):
   L=2*n-1
   target = np.zeros(L)
   # flat desired c with max perhaps average? sum n^2
   target[:] = n*n / L  # sum n^2
   Y = np.fft.rfft(target, N)
   # For each freq, compute sqrt of Y with phase
   # Y complex; avoid zero
   amp = np.sqrt(np.abs(Y))
   phase = 0.5 * np.angle(Y)
   X = amp * np.exp(1j*phase)
   # Symmetrize for real inverse: X[k] = 0.5*(X[k] + conj(X[N-k])) for k internal, X[0],X[N/2] real.
   X = symmetrize_fft(X,N)
   a0 = np.fft.irfft(X, N)[:n]
   # Try several random phase multipliers? maybe not.
   a1 = np.abs(a0)
   a2 = a0 if min>... 
   return normalize(a1)
```

```
def run_optimizer(a0, budget, seed, N=None, verbose=True):
    if budget <=0 or len(a0)==0: return a0, inf
    n=len(a0)
    if N is None: N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    a=normalize_sequence(a0, float(n))
    c, fa = self_conv_fft(a,N)
    c = np.maximum(c,0.0)
    S=a.sum()
    best_a=a.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_score=score_from_values(2*n*best_c.max()/(S*S))
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
    eta=0.1
    no_improve=0; iter_count=0
    # pool second best
    second_a=None; second_score=inf
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2:
        prog = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/max(budget,1e-6))
        # Ensure a finite
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): a=best_a.copy()
        c, fa = self_conv_fft(a,N)
        c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
        S=a.sum()
        max_c=float(np.max(c))
        score = 2.0*n*max_c/(S*S) if S>0.01 else inf
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); no_improduce=0
        elif no_improve==0 and score < second_score -1e-12:
            second_score=score; second_a=a.copy()
        # choose weights
        # if score not finite break?
        w = blended_weights(c, prog, rng)
        grad = correlation_gradient(a,w,N,fa)
        # if grad not finite: try var weights
        ... line search
        improved=False
        if finite grad:
            cand_a, cand_score, improved = line_search(a, grad, N, eta, S)
            if improved:
                a=cand_a; eta=min(0.8, eta*1.2); no_improve=0
            else:
                eta=max(0.005, eta*0.75); no_improve +=1
                # try perturbation
                cand = random_perturb(a, rng, n, 0.1+0.1*no_improve)
                c2=... score2; if score2<score: a=cand; no_improve=0
                else:
                   # crossover best/second
                   if second_a is not None:
                       cand = normalize_sequence(np.sqrt(np.maximum(a,0)*np.maximum(second_a,0)+1e-12), S)
                       score2=...; if improve: ...
                       cand = normalize_sequence(0.5*a+0.5*second_a,S)
                   if no_improve > 80:
                       # jump
                       a = normalize_sequence(best_a * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.3,n)), best_a.sum())
                       no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve +=1
        iter_count +=1
        if verbose and iter_count%200==0: print(...)
    # final verify best
    return best_a, best_score
```

```
def line_search(a, grad, N, eta, base_score, maxlog=6.0):
    S=a.sum()
    best_score=base_score; best_a=a
    etas=[eta*0.25, eta*0.5, eta, eta*2.0, eta*4.0]
    for e in etas:
       cand = multiplicative_update(a, grad, e, S)
       sc = score_a(cand,N)
       if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=sc; best_a=cand
    return best_a, best_score, best_score < base_score - 1e-12
```

```
def score_a(a,N=None):
   n=len(a)
   if N is None: N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
   c=self_conv_fft(a,N)[0]
   S=a.sum()
   if S<0.01: return inf
   return 2.0*n*float(np.max(c))/(S*S)
```

```
def random_perturb(a, rng, n, strength=0.1):
   cand=a.copy()
   # global small
   cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, strength*0.2, n))
   # blocks
   nblocks = rng.integers(2,6)
   for _ in range(nblocks):
       w = int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//10)))
       s = int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-w)))
       cand[s:s+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, strength, w))
   return normalize_sequence(cand, a.sum())
```

Generate inits for main n:
```
def make_parent_interp(parent, n):
    parent = np.clip(parent,0,1000)
    m=parent.size
    if m==1: return np.full(n,parent[0])
    x_old = np.linspace(0,1,m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0,1,n)
    y = np.interp(x_new, x_old, parent)
    # ensure positive min
    y = np.maximum(y, 1e-6)
    return normalize_sequence(y, n)
```

```
def init_fourier_flat(n,N,seed):
    L=2*n-1
    target=np.full(L, (float(n)*n)/L)
    Y=np.fft.rfft(target,N)
    # variant 1: sqrt direct
    X=np.sqrt(np.abs(Y)) * np.exp(1j*0.5*np.angle(Y))
    X=symmetrize_complex_fft(X,N)
    a=np.fft.irfft(X,N)[:n]
    a=np.abs(a)
    # variant 2: amplitudes with random phases maybe take best of a few? We can just choose one.
    if a.sum()>0: return normalize_sequence(a,n)
    return np.ones(n)
```

`symmetrize_complex_fft(X,N)`: top-level:
```
def symmetrize_complex_fft(X,N):
    X = np.asarray(X, complex)
    if N%2==0:
       X[0] = float(X[0].real)
       X[N//2] = float(X[N//2].real)
       for k in range(1,N//2):
           X[k] = 0.5*(X[k] + np.conj(X[N-k]))
    else:
       X[0] = float(X[0].real)
       for k in range(1,(N+1)//2):
           X[k] = 0.5*(X[k] + np.conj(X[N-k]))
    return X
```
`np.fft.irfft` expects rfft shape N//2+1. Our X length N//2+1. For sym, internal. Good.

But sqrt of Y with phase may produce X with negative Imag; sym may distort. Still init.

Let's refine `blended_weights` to be efficient:
```
def blended_weights(c, progress, rng):
    L=c.size; cmax=float(np.max(c)); mean=float(np.mean(c))
    # top fraction
    f = 0.5 * (0.01/0.5) ** progress
    # jitter f: rng.uniform(f*0.5, f*1.5) maybe
    f = min(0.9, max(0.002, f * rng.uniform(0.5,1.5)))
    tau = adaptive_tau(c, f)
    # softmax top
    e = np.exp((c - cmax)/tau)
    es = e.sum()
    if es > 0: w_top = e/es
    else: w_top = np.ones(L)/L
    # flat
    if rng.random() < 0.5:
       w = c - mean
       s = np.max(np.abs(w))
       if s > 0: w_flat = w/s
       else: w_flat = np.ones(L)/L
    else:
       w = np.maximum(c,0)
       s=w.sum()
       if s>0: w_flat = w/s
       else: w_flat = np.ones(L)/L
    alpha = 0.1 + 0.9 * progress
    alpha = min(1.0, max(0.0, alpha * rng.uniform(0.8,1.2)))
    w = alpha * w_top + (1.0-alpha) * w_flat
    # If all close zero, fallback
    maxabs = np.max(np.abs(w))
    if maxabs <= 1e-30: w = w_top
    else: w = w / maxabs  # scale to maxabs1, helps gradient scale
    return w
```
Since softmax sum=1, if maxabs maybe 1/K (small for uniform top). Scaling to maxabs=1 increases gradient by factor K, but eta line search handles? It may make step sizes smaller. Better not arbitrary scale. But eta adaptive based on gradient mean abs, so scale cancels in multiplicative_update (divides by mean abs). So not matter. But for signed flat, scale to maxabs1 okay. Scaling all weights doesn't change gradient direction; eta through scale normalization inside update cancels direction? Wait multiplicative_update divides grad by its mean abs, so any scalar weight scaling cancels. Thus weight scaling irrelevant. Good. We can leave.

`adaptive_tau` if cmax is zero? handle.

`correlation_gradient` output maybe large; `multiplicative_update` uses mean abs. If gradient has all same, scale zero; no step.

So subtracting mean (unweighted) is equivalent to subtracting a constant. Good. It may affect if we normalize to same sum but constant cancels exactly. So okay.

Let's implement `distribute_deficit` inside `normalize_sequence` top-level. No inner functions.

```
def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum, cap=1000.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0: return a
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        # uniform
        val = target_sum / a.size
        if val > cap:
            val = cap
        out = np.full(a.size, val, dtype=np.float64)
        # if target_sum > capacity, fill cap? But objective inf? We'll return cap values; sum may <target.
        return out
    a = a * (target_sum / s)
    # cap and redistribute
    if a.size and a.max() > cap + 1e-12:
        a = np.minimum(a, cap)
        for _ in range(8):
            deficit = target_sum - a.sum()
            if deficit <= 1e-9:
                break
            if deficit < 0:
                s = a.sum()
                if s > 0:
                    a *= (-deficit / s)  # scale down; max remains <=cap
                    # Re-check cap? scaling down no problem
                break
            mask = a < cap - 1e-12
            if not mask.any():
                break
            add = deficit / float(mask.sum())
            a[mask] = np.minimum(a[mask] + add, cap)
    else:
        s=a.sum()
        if s <=0: ...
    # If after scaling down, ensure sum maybe target? ok.
    return a
```

`multiplicative_update` and `line_search`:
```
def line_search(a, grad, N, eta, base_score, max_log=6.0):
    S = float(a.sum())
    best_score = base_score
    best_a = a
    if S <= 0: return a, base_score, False
    etas = (eta*0.25, eta*0.75, eta, eta*2.0, eta*4.0) # 5 maybe
    for e in etas:
       if e <=0: continue
       cand = multiplicative_update(a, grad, e, S, max_log)
       sc = score_array(cand, N)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=sc; best_a=cand
    return best_a, best_score, best_score < base_score - 1e-12
```
5 candidates might be too many; use 4. We can also break if e*4 candidate improves? We still test all. Use 4: [0.5,1,2,4] times eta.

```
def run_optimizer(...):
    ...
    while time < deadline:
       # if a diverged, reset to best
       c, fa = self_convolution_fft(a,N)
       c = np.maximum(c,0)
       S = a.sum()
       if S <= 0.01: a=best_a; continue
       max_c = c.max()
       score = 2.0*n*max_c/(S*S)
       if score < best_score - 1e-12: ...
       if not finite score: a=best_a.copy(); no_improvable=...; continue
       # maybe if no improvement long, reset a to best with perturbation
       if no_improve > 200:
           a = random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.25)
           no_improve=0
           continue
       w = blended_weights(c, prog, rng)
       grad = correlation_gradient(a,w,N,fa)
       if not finite grad:
          no_improve +=1; continue
       cand_a, cand_score, improved = line_search(a, grad, N, eta, score)
       if improved:
           a = cand_a
           eta = min(0.8, eta* (1.1 + 0.2*prog?))
           no_improve=0
       else:
           eta = max(0.003, eta*0.8)
           no_improve +=1
           # perturbation trial if no_improve % 10 ==0
           if no_improve >= 10 and no_improve % 10 == 0:
               strength = 0.05 + 0.05*min(1, no_improve/100)
               candp = random_perturb(a, rng, strength)
               scp = score_array(candp,N)
               if scp < score:
                   a = candp; no_improve=0
               else:
                   # try crossover best and a or second
                   if np.array_equal(a,best_a):
                      candc = random_perturb(best_a,rng, strength*1.5)
                      ...
                   else:
                      candc = normalize_sequence(np.sqrt(np.maximum(a,0)*np.maximum(best_a,0)+1e-12), a.sum())
                      ... after arith
```

```
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
            return None
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.sum() <=0:
            return None
        return arr
    except Exception:
        return None
```
References global variable; okay.

`interp_parent(parent,n)`:
```
def interp_parent(parent,n):
    m=parent.size
    if m==n: return parent.copy()
    if m==1: return np.full(n,parent[0])
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,parent)
    return y
```
Normalize outside.

Make inits: For parent length, parent itself normalized. For main, interpolated.

Let's write `build_inits(n, parent, seed)`:
```
def build_inits(n, parent, seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    inits=[]
    if parent is not None and parent.size>0:
       inits.append(('parent', interp_parent(parent,n)))
       p=interp_parent(parent,n)
       inits.append(('parent_sym', (p+p[::-1])*0.5))
    inits.append(('ones', np.ones(n)))
    inits.append(('edge50', make_edge_power(n,0.5,0.5)))
    inits.append(('edge100', make_edge_power(n,1.0,1.0)))
    inits.append(('cos', make_cosine_edge(n,0.8)))
    inits.append(('quad', make_quadratic_edge(n,1.0)))
    inits.append(('gauss', make_gaussian(n,0.25)))
    inits.append(('rand1', make_random_positive(n,rng,0.35)))
    inits.append(('rand2', make_random_positive(n,rng,0.7)))
    inits.append(('symrand', make_symmetric_random(n,rng,0.4)))
    N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    inits.append(('fourier', make_fourier_flat(n,N,seed)))
    # normalize each? Could do in optimize_length, but build can return raw.
    return inits
```
Too many inits 13; scoring all maybe ok. Use top 4.

```
half = (n+1)//2
left = np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,half))
if n%2==0: a=np.concatenate([left, left[::-1]])
else: a=np.concatenate([left, left[-2::-1]])
return a
```

Now, `optimize_length` details:
```
def optimize_length(n, parent, budget, seed, results, print_func=None):
    if budget <= 1 or n<=0: return
    N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    inits = build_inits(n,parent,seed)
    scored=[]
    for name,a in inits:
       a = normalize_sequence(a, float(n))
       if a is None: continue
       sc = score_array(a,N)
       if np.isfinite(sc): scored.append((sc,a))
    if not scored:
       a=np.ones(n); scored=[(2.0,a)]
    scored.sort(key=score_first)
    max_opt = min(4, len(scored))
    fracs=[0.5,0.25,0.15,0.1]
    total_frac=sum(fracs[:max_opt])
    end_time=time.monotonic()+budget
    for i in range(max_opt):
       b = budget * fracs[i] / total_frac
       if i>0 and time.monotonic() > end_time - 5: break
       a=scored[i][1]
       # If i>0, maybe slightly perturb to avoid identical? no.
       res_a, res_sc = run_optimizer(a,b,seed+i*777+ int(n),N, progress_start=0.0, eta_init=0.12)
       results.append((res_sc,res_a))
       # Also maybe keep initial if better? run_optimizer should at least initial.
```

Let's code `run_optimizer` carefully:
```
def run_optimizer(a0, budget, seed, N=None, verbose=False, progress_start=0.0, eta_init=0.12):
    if len(a0)==0: return a0, inf
    n=len(a0)
    if N is None: N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    a = normalize_sequence(a0, float(n))
    c = self_convolution_fft(a,N)[0]
    S = a.sum()
    base_score = 2.0*n*max(c)/(S*S)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score=base_score
    if budget <= 0: return best_a,best_score
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    eta=eta_init
    no_improve=0; it=0; last_print=start
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
           a=best_a.copy(); no_improve=0
       c,_ = self_convolution_fft(a,N)
       c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
       S=float(a.sum())
       if S <= 0.01:
           a=best_a.copy(); no_improve=0; continue
       max_c=float(np.max(c))
       score = 2.0*n*max_c/(S*S)
       if not np.isfinite(score):
           a=best_a.copy(); no_improve=0; continue
       if score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=score; best_a=a.copy(); no_improve=0
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-start
       prog = progress_start + (1.0-progress_start)*min(1.0, elapsed/max(budget,1e-9))
       # Check improvement even if score not better than current? line search uses score base
       w = blended_weights(c, prog, rng)
       grad = correlation_gradient(a,w,N)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve > 20:
               a = random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.2)
               no_improve=0
           continue
       cand_a, cand_score, improved = line_search(a, grad, N, eta, score)
       if improved:
           a = cand_a
           eta = min(0.9, eta * 1.25)
           no_improve=0
       else:
           eta = max(0.004, eta * 0.75)
           no_improve +=1
           # perturbation triggers
           if no_improve == 5 or no_improve == 10 or no_improve == 20 or no_improve <= 50 and no_improve % 5 ==0:
               strength = 0.04 + 0.02*min(1.0, no_improve/50.0)
               candp = random_perturb(a, rng, strength)
               scp = score_array(candp,N)
               if np.isfinite(scp) and scp < score:
                   a=candp; no_improve=0
           if no_improve >= 30 and no_improve % 30 ==0:
               # symmetric breaking with best
               for delta in (0.02,0.05,0.1):
                   cand = antisymmetric_perturb(a, delta)
                   scp=score_array(cand,N)
                   if scp < score:
                       a=cand; no_improve=0; break
           if no_improve > 200:
               a = random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.3)
               no_improve=0
       it +=1
       now=time.monotonic()
       if verbose and now - last_print > 15.0:
           last_print=now
           print(f"    opt n={n} it={it} best={best_score:.6f} cur={score:.6f} eta={eta:.4f} noimp={no_improve}")
    # final exact best
    cbest = self_convolution_fft(best_a,N)[0]
    Sbest=best_a.sum()
    if Sbest>0.01:
       best_score = min(best_score, 2.0*n*float(np.max(np.maximum(cbest,0)))/(Sbest*Sbest))
    return best_a, best_score
```

```
def antisymmetric_perturb(a, delta):
    cand=a.copy()
    if cand.size >1:
       cand[0::2] *= (1+delta)
       cand[1::2] *= (1-delta)
    return normalize_sequence(cand, float(a.sum()))
```

```
def coordinate_polish(a, budget, N, seed):
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
   rng=...
   a=normalize_sequence(a,float(len(a)))
   best=score_array(a,N); best_a=a
   it=0
   factors=(0.85,0.9,0.95,1.05,1.1,1.15)
   while time<deadline-2:
      # compute gradient direction with top weights to choose important indices? use random to save.
      idx = int(rng.integers(0, len(a)))
      best_cand = None; best_sc=best
      for t in factors:
         cand=a.copy(); cand[idx]*=t; cand=normalize_sequence(cand,float(a.sum()))
         sc=score_array(cand,N)
         if sc<best_sc-1e-12: best_sc=sc; best_cand=cand
      if best_cand is not None:
         a=best_cand; best=best_sc
      else:
         # occasionally random block
         if it%5==0: random perturb small
      it+=1
   return best_a,best
```

Let's implement `normalize_sequence` robustly:
```
def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum, cap=1000.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
       a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
       return a
    a = a.copy()
    mask_finite = np.isfinite(a)
    a[~mask_finite] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    ts = float(target_sum)
    if ts <= 0.0:
       return a
    if s <= 1e-300:
       val = ts / float(a.size)
       if val > cap:
           val = cap
       out = np.full(a.size, val, dtype=np.float64)
       # if total capacity less than ts? fill cap all
       return out
    a *= (ts / s)
    if a.size >0:
       maxv = float(a.max())
       if maxv > cap:
           a = np.minimum(a, cap)
           for _ in range(10):
               current = float(a.sum())
               deficit = ts - current
               if deficit <= 1e-9:
                   break
               if deficit < 0.0:
                   if current > 0:
                       a *= (-deficit / current)
                   break
               mask = a < cap - 1e-12
               if not np.any(mask):
                   break
               add = deficit / float(np.count_nonzero(mask))
               a[mask] = np.minimum(a[mask] + add, cap)
    return a
```
If after scaling down, max <=cap. If target_sum > cap*len, mask all cap, deficit positive, breaks; sum <ts. Fine.

Note: In `random_perturb`, target_sum = float(a.sum()); if a has cap? fine.

`self_convolution_fft`:
```
def self_convolution_fft(a, N):
    n = len(a)
    L = 2*n - 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    c = c[:L]
    return c, fa
```
No clip. In score, clip max? Since a nonnegative, c nonnegative. But for max, can use `np.max(c)`. Negative tiny could not affect max unless all negative impossible. Safer `np.maximum(c,0.0)` before max.

`score_array`:
```
def score_array(a, N=None):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0: return float('inf')
    if N is None: N = next_pow2(2*n-1)
    c,_ = self_convolution_fft(a,N)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01: return float('inf')
    cmax = float(np.max(np.maximum(c,0.0)))
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (s*s))
```

`correlation_gradient`:
```
def correlation_gradient(a, w, N, fa=None):
    n = len(a)
    if fa is None: fa = np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    r = np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fa) * fw, N)
    g = 2.0 * r[:n]
    return g
```
If w length <N, rfft pads. If w length L. Good.

`adaptive_tau`:
```
def adaptive_tau(c, f_top):
    L = len(c)
    if L <= 0: return 1.0
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if f_top <= 0: f_top=1e-4
    if f_top >= 0.999: f_top=0.999
    idx = int(round(L * (1.0 - f_top)))
    if idx < 1: idx=1
    if idx >= L: idx=L-1
    q = float(np.partition(c, idx)[idx])
    gap = cmax - q
    if gap <= 1e-12:
        gap = max(1e-9, cmax * 1e-8)
    tau = gap / max(1e-9, -math.log(f_top))
    return max(1e-8, tau)
```
If cmax huge, gap maybe 1e-5, tau 1e-8 min. ok.

`make_top_weights`:
```
def make_top_weights(c, tau):
    L=len(c); cmax=float(np.max(c))
    if tau <=0: tau=1e-8
    e = np.exp((c-cmax)/tau)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       out = np.zeros(L); imax = np.argmax(c); out[imax]=1.0; return out
    return e/s
```

`make_flat_weights(c, rng)`:
```
def make_flat_weights(c, rng):
    L=len(c)
    if rng.random() < 0.6:
       cc = np.maximum(c,0.0)
       s=float(cc.sum())
       if s>0: return cc/s
       return np.ones(L)/L
    else:
       mean=float(np.mean(c))
       w=c-mean
       s=float(np.max(np.abs(w)))
       if s>0: return w/s
       return np.ones(L)/L
```

`blended_weights`:
```
def blended_weights(c, progress, rng):
    L=len(c)
    if progress < 0.08:
       alpha=0.0
    else:
       alpha = 0.1 + 0.9*progress
       alpha = alpha * rng.uniform(0.8,1.2)
       alpha = min(1.0, max(0.0, alpha))
    f_top = 0.5 * (0.008/0.5) ** max(0.0, min(1.0, progress))
    f_top *= rng.uniform(0.6,1.5)
    f_top = min(0.95, max(0.002, f_top))
    tau = adaptive_tau(c, f_top)
    w_top = make_top_weights(c, tau)
    w_flat = make_flat_weights(c, rng)
    w = alpha * w_top + (1.0-alpha) * w_flat
    s = float(np.max(np.abs(w)))
    if s <= 1e-30:
       return w_top
    return w
```
Scaling not change direction; okay.

Potential issue: For early progress<0.08, alpha=0, w_flat can be negative; gradient direction. Good.

`multiplicative_update`:
```
def multiplicative_update(a, grad, eta, target_sum, max_log=6.0):
    n = len(a)
    S = float(target_sum)
    if S <= 0: S = float(a.sum())
    mean = float(np.mean(grad))
    g = grad - mean
    scale = float(np.mean(np.abs(g)))
    if scale <= 1e-30:
       return a.copy()
    z = -eta * g / scale
    z = np.clip(z, -max_log, max_log)
    cand = a * np.exp(z)
    return normalize_sequence(cand, S)
```
If grad all equal but mean computed, g zeros. Good. If eta very large, eta/scale? scale cancels? Actually g/scale O(1), eta up to 0.9, z±0.9 not too large. max_log not needed but fine. If eta=0.9, z small. To allow big jumps during stall, eta max 0.9 only small log step. Multiplicative with mean-abs-normalized gives log changes ≤η. If η=0.9, max relative change exp(0.9)=2.46. Fine. Stall random perturb uses stronger.

Maybe eta should be up to 1.5 for faster. Set max 1.5. In run_optimizer `eta=min(1.5, eta*1.25)`. Initial 0.2.

`line_search` with etas [eta*0.25, eta*0.75, eta, eta*2, eta*4] can produce eta>1.5 if eta max 1.5 => 6.0; log clip max 6.0 (relative e^6 400) could be too aggressive; maybe max_log=4. Use max_log=5. For line search, aggressive can find jumps. But normalization after; okay. Set max_log=5. Eta candidates up to 4*1.5=6, clipped log 5 -> relative 148. Could be chaotic. Maybe cap eta candidates at 2.0. In line_search: `etas = [eta*0.25, eta*0.66, eta, min(2.0, eta*2.0)]`. Use 4. Good.

`line_search`:
```
def line_search(a, grad, N, eta, base_score, max_log=5.0):
    S = float(a.sum())
    best_score = base_score
    best_a = a
    candidates = (eta*0.25, eta*0.67, eta, min(2.0, eta*2.0))
    last = None
    for e in candidates:
       if e <=0: continue
       if last is not None and abs(e-last) < 1e-12: continue
       cand = multiplicative_update(a, grad, e, S, max_log)
       sc = score_array(cand, N)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=sc; best_a=cand
       last=e
    return best_a, best_score, best_score < base_score - 1e-12
```
No need last.

`random_perturb`:
```
def random_perturb(a,rng,strength=0.1):
    n=len(a); S=float(a.sum())
    cand=a.copy()
    if S <=0: return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), float(n))
    cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, max(0.0, strength)*0.2, n))
    nblocks = int(rng.integers(2,6))
    for _ in range(nblocks):
       wmin = max(1, n//500)
       wmax = max(wmin+1, n//8)
       width = int(rng.integers(wmin, wmax))
       start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width)))
       cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, strength, width))
    return normalize_sequence(cand, S)
```
If width > n, start range; OK.

`antisymmetric_perturb`:
```
def antisymmetric_perturb(a, delta):
    cand=a.copy()
    if cand.size > 1:
       low = max(0.0, 1.0 - delta)
       high = 1.0 + delta
       cand[0::2] *= high
       cand[1::2] *= low
    return normalize_sequence(cand, float(a.sum()))
```

```
def make_edge_power(n, p, alpha):
    i=np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    d=np.minimum(i, n-1.0-i)
    a = 1.0 / np.power(d + alpha, p)
    return a
```
If p=0.5, alpha=0.5 edges ~1.4, center ~0.0035, very edge-heavy. Maybe too extreme; but normalize. Could produce bad. Use p=0.5 and p=0.2? Let's use less extreme: p=0.5 alpha=1.0, p=0.25 alpha=1.0. Or p=1 alpha=5. We'll include a few.

```
def optimize_length(n, parent, budget, seed, results, verbose=False):
    if n <=0 or budget <=2: return
    N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    inits=build_inits(n,parent,seed)
    scored=[]
    for name,a in inits:
       try:
          a=normalize_sequence(a, float(n))
          sc=score_array(a,N)
       except Exception:
          continue
       if np.isfinite(sc): scored.append((sc,a))
    if not scored:
       a=np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64); scored=[(2.0,a)]
    scored.sort(key=score_first)
    if verbose:
       tops = []
       for i in range(min(3,len(scored))): tops.append(f"{scored[i][0]:.6f}")
       print(f"optimize_length n={n} best_init=" + " ".join(tops))
    fracs=(0.5,0.25,0.15,0.1)
    k=min(4,len(scored))
    total_fr=sum(fracs[:k])
    end=time.monotonic()+budget
    for i in range(k):
       if time.monotonic() > end - 2: break
       b=budget * fracs[i] / total_fr
       if b < 2: continue
       a=scored[i][1]
       try:
           res_a,res_sc=run_optimizer(a,b,int(seed)+i*131071,N,verbose=verbose and i==0, progress_start=0.0, eta_init=0.15)
       except Exception:
           res_a=res_sc=a=a, sc=scored[i][0]???
       results.append((res_sc,res_a))
```

```python
"""
Optimize nonnegative step-function coefficients to minimize
J(a) = 2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2.

The search uses:
- FFT-accelerated exact scores and gradients for many random/structured modes,
- adaptive log-sum-exp "top coefficients" weights mixed with variance/L2 flatness weights,
- multiplicative (mirror) line search with adaptive step size,
- symmetry-breaking perturbations and crossovers,
- multi-start over lengths, including the parent construction when useful,
- final polishing and verification with the provided evaluate_sequence.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


TARGET_LENGTH = 8192


def score_first(x):
    return x[0]


def next_pow2(m):
    s = 1
    while s < m:
        s <<= 1
    return s


def load_parent_sequence():
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(arr.sum()) <= 0.0:
        return None
    return arr


def normalize_sequence(a, target_sum, cap=1000.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a = a.copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)

    ts = float(target_sum)
    if ts <= 0.0:
        return a
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        val = ts / float(a.size)
        if val > cap:
            val = cap
        return np.full(a.size, val, dtype=np.float64)

    a *= (ts / s)

    if a.size > 0:
        maxv = float(a.max())
        if maxv > cap:
            a = np.minimum(a, cap)
            for _ in range(12):
                current = float(a.sum())
                deficit = ts - current
                if deficit <= 1e-9:
                    break
                if deficit < 0.0:
                    if current > 0.0:
                        a *= (-deficit / current)
                    break
                mask = a < (cap - 1e-12)
                if not np.any(mask):
                    break
                add = deficit / float(np.count_nonzero(mask))
                a[mask] = np.minimum(a[mask] + add, cap)

    floor = max(1e-15, 1e-13 * ts / float(a.size))
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    s2 = float(a.sum())
    if s2 > ts and s2 > 0.0:
        a *= (ts / s2)

    return a


def self_convolution_fft(a, N):
    n = len(a)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    c = c[:L]
    return c, fa


def score_array(a, N=None):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    if N is None:
        N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    c, _ = self_convolution_fft(a, N)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    cmax = float(np.max(np.maximum(c, 0.0)))
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (s * s))


def correlation_gradient(a, w, N, fa=None):
    n = len(a)
    if fa is None:
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    r = np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fa) * fw, N)
    return 2.0 * r[:n]


def make_top_weights(c, tau, cmax=None):
    L = len(c)
    if L == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if cmax is None:
        cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if tau <= 0.0:
        tau = 1e-8
    e = np.exp((c - cmax) / tau)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        out = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        out[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return out
    return e / s


def make_flat_weights(c, rng):
    L = len(c)
    if rng.random() < 0.60:
        cc = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        s = float(cc.sum())
        if s > 0.0:
            return cc / s
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)
    else:
        mean = float(np.mean(c))
        w = c - mean
        s = float(np.max(np.abs(w)))
        if s > 0.0:
            return w / s
        return np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64) / float(L)


def blended_weights(c, progress, rng, cmax=None):
    L = len(c)
    if L == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if cmax is None:
        cmax = float(np.max(c))
    cmean = float(np.mean(c))
    gap = cmax - cmean
    if gap <= 1e-12:
        gap = max(1e-8, abs(cmax) * 1e-8)

    if progress < 0.08:
        alpha = 0.0
    else:
        alpha = 0.1 + 0.9 * progress
        alpha = alpha * rng.uniform(0.8, 1.2)
        alpha = min(1.0, max(0.0, alpha))

    f_top = 0.5 * (0.008 / 0.5) ** max(0.0, min(1.0, progress))
    f_top *= rng.uniform(0.7, 1.4)
    f_top = min(0.95, max(0.002, f_top))

    tau = max(1e-8, gap / max(1e-9, -math.log(f_top)))

    w_top = make_top_weights(c, tau, cmax)
    w_flat = make_flat_weights(c, rng)
    w = alpha * w_top + (1.0 - alpha) * w_flat

    m = float(np.max(np.abs(w)))
    if m <= 1e-30:
        return w_top
    return w


def multiplicative_update(a, grad, eta, target_sum, max_log=5.0):
    S = float(target_sum)
    if S <= 0.0:
        S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 0.0:
        return a.copy()

    mean = float(np.mean(grad))
    g = grad - mean
    scale = float(np.mean(np.abs(g)))
    if scale <= 1e-30:
        return a.copy()

    z = -eta * g / scale
    z = np.clip(z, -max_log, max_log)
    cand = a * np.exp(z)
    return normalize_sequence(cand, S)


def line_search(a, grad, N, eta, base_score):
    S = float(a.sum())
    best_score = base_score
    best_a = a
    etas = (eta * 0.25, eta * 0.67, eta, min(2.0, eta * 2.0))
    last_eta = None
    for e in etas:
        if e <= 0.0:
            continue
        if last_eta is not None and abs(e - last_eta) < 1e-12:
            continue
        cand = multiplicative_update(a, grad, e, S, 5.0)
        sc = score_array(cand, N)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = cand
        last_eta = e
    return best_a, best_score, best_score < base_score - 1e-12


def random_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.1):
    n = len(a)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 0.0:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), float(n))

    cand = a.copy()
    st = max(0.0, float(strength))
    if st > 0.0:
        cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, st * 0.2, n))
        nblocks = int(rng.integers(2, 6))
        for _ in range(nblocks):
            wmin = max(1, n // 500)
            wmax = max(wmin + 1, n // 8)
            width = int(rng.integers(wmin, wmax))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width)))
            cand[start:start + width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, st, width))
    return normalize_sequence(cand, S)


def antisymmetric_perturb(a, delta):
    cand = a.copy()
    if cand.size > 1:
        high = 1.0 + delta
        low = max(0.0, 1.0 - delta)
        cand[0::2] *= high
        cand[1::2] *= low
    return normalize_sequence(cand, float(a.sum()))


def run_optimizer(a0, budget, seed, N=None, verbose=False, progress_start=0.0, eta_init=0.15):
    if a0 is None or len(a0) == 0 or budget <= 0.0:
        return a0, float("inf")

    n = len(a0)
    if N is None:
        N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)

    a = normalize_sequence(a0, float(n))
    base_sum = float(a.sum())
    if base_sum > 0.0:
        tiny = max(1e-8, 1e-8 * float(np.max(a)))
        a = a + tiny
        a = normalize_sequence(a, base_sum)

    c, _ = self_convolution_fft(a, N)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 0.01:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        S = float(n)
        c, _ = self_convolution_fft(a, N)
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(c)) / (S * S))

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    eta = max(0.005, float(eta_init))
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = start

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
            a = best_a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        c, fa = self_convolution_fft(a, N)
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        S = float(a.sum())
        if S <= 0.01:
            a = best_a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        max_c = float(np.max(c))
        score = float(2.0 * n * max_c / (S * S))
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            a = best_a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = score
            best_a = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0

        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        prog = progress_start + (1.0 - progress_start) * min(1.0, elapsed / max(1e-9, float(budget)))
        prog = max(0.0, min(1.0, prog))

        w = blended_weights(c, prog, rng, max_c)
        grad = correlation_gradient(a, w, N, fa)

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 20:
                a = random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.2)
                no_improve = 0
            continue

        cand_a, cand_score, improved = line_search(a, grad, N, eta, score)

        if improved:
            a = cand_a
            eta = min(1.5, eta * 1.25)
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            eta = max(0.004, eta * 0.75)
            no_improve += 1

            trigger = (no_improve == 5 or no_improve == 10 or no_improve == 20 or
                       (no_improve <= 60 and no_improve % 5 == 0))
            if trigger:
                strength = 0.04 + 0.03 * min(1.0, no_improve / 50.0)
                candp = random_perturb(a, rng, strength)
                scp = score_array(candp, N)
                if np.isfinite(scp) and scp < score:
                    a = candp
                    no_improve = 0
                    etg = 0.15

            if no_improve >= 30 and no_improve % 30 == 0:
                for delta in (0.02, 0.05, 0.10):
                    cand = antisymmetric_perturb(a, delta)
                    scp = score_array(cand, N)
                    if np.isfinite(scp) and scp < score:
                        a = cand
                        no_improve = 0
                        break

            if np.array_equal(a, best_a) and no_improve >= 20:
                cand = random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.25)
                scp = score_array(cand, N)
                if np.isfinite(scp) and scp < best_score:
                    a = cand
                    best_a = a.copy()
                    best_score = scp
                    no_improve = 0

            if no_improve > 220:
                a = random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.30)
                no_improve = 0

        it += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        if verbose and now - last_print > 18.0:
            last_print = now
            print(f"    opt n={n} it={it} best={best_score:.6f} cur={score:.6f} eta={eta:.4f} noimp={no_improve}")

    cbest, _ = self_convolution_fft(best_a, N)
    cbest = np.maximum(cbest, 0.0)
    Sbest = float(best_a.sum())
    if Sbest > 0.01:
        final_score = float(2.0 * n * float(np.max(cbest)) / (Sbest * Sbest))
        if np.isfinite(final_score) and final_score < best_score:
            best_score = final_score

    return best_a, best_score


def interp_parent(parent, n):
    m = parent.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return parent.copy()
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(parent[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, parent)


def make_edge_power(n, p, alpha):
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    d = np.minimum(i, float(n - 1) - i)
    return 1.0 / np.power(d + alpha, p)


def make_cosine_edge(n, A):
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    a = 1.0 + A * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * t)
    return np.maximum(a, 0.01)


def make_quadratic_edge(n, A):
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    t = 2.0 * np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n) - 1.0
    return 1.0 + A * t * t


def make_gaussian(n, sigma_frac=0.25):
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) - (n - 1) / 2.0
    sigma = max(1e-6, sigma_frac * (n - 1))
    return np.exp(-x * x / (2.0 * sigma * sigma))


def make_random_positive(n, rng, sigma):
    return np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))


def make_symmetric_random(n, rng, sigma):
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    half = (n + 1) // 2
    left = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, half))
    if n % 2 == 0:
        return np.concatenate((left, left[::-1]))
    return np.concatenate((left, left[-2::-1]))


def symmetrize_complex_fft(X, N):
    X = np.asarray(X, dtype=np.complex128)
    if N % 2 == 0:
        X[0] = float(X[0].real)
        X[N // 2] = float(X[N // 2].real)
        for k in range(1, N // 2):
            X[k] = 0.5 * (X[k] + np.conj(X[N - k]))
    else:
        X[0] = float(X[0].real)
        for k in range(1, (N + 1) // 2):
            X[k] = 0.5 * (X[k] + np.conj(X[N - k]))
    return X


def make_fourier_flat(n, N, seed):
    L = 2 * n - 1
    target = np.full(L, (float(n) * n) / float(L), dtype=np.float64)
    Y = np.fft.rfft(target, N)
    amp = np.sqrt(np.abs(Y))
    phase = 0.5 * np.angle(Y)
    X = amp * np.exp(1j * phase)
    X = symmetrize_complex_fft(X, N)
    a = np.fft.irfft(X, N)[:n]
    a = np.abs(a)
    if float(a.sum()) <= 0.0:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return a


def build_inits(n, parent, seed):
    if n <= 0:
        return []
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    inits = []

    if parent is not None and parent.size > 0:
        p = interp_parent(parent, n)
        inits.append(("parent", p))
        inits.append(("parent_sym", 0.5 * (p + p[::-1])))

    inits.append(("ones", np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))
    inits.append(("edgeA", make_edge_power(n, 0.5, 1.0)))
    inits.append(("edgeB", make_edge_power(n, 0.25, 1.0)))
    inits.append(("cos", make_cosine_edge(n, 0.8)))
    inits.append(("quad", make_quadratic_edge(n, 1.0)))
    inits.append(("gauss", make_gaussian(n, 0.25)))
    inits.append(("gauss_wide", make_gaussian(n, 0.45)))
    inits.append(("rand1", make_random_positive(n, rng, 0.35)))
    inits.append(("rand2", make_random_positive(n, rng, 0.75)))
    inits.append(("symrand", make_symmetric_random(n, rng, 0.45)))

    N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    inits.append(("fourier", make_fourier_flat(n, N, seed + 7)))

    return inits


def optimize_length(n, parent, budget, seed, results, verbose=False):
    if n <= 0 or budget <= 2.0:
        return
    n = int(n)
    N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    inits = build_inits(n, parent, seed)

    scored = []
    for name, a in inits:
        try:
            av = normalize_sequence(a, float(n))
            if av.size != n:
                continue
            sc = score_array(av, N)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((sc, av))
        except Exception:
            continue

    if not scored:
        scored.append((2.0, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))

    scored.sort(key=score_first)

    if verbose:
        top_desc = " ".join([f"{scored[i][0]:.6f}" for i in range(min(3, len(scored)))])
        print(f"optimize_length n={n} top_init_scores={top_desc}")

    fracs = (0.50, 0.25, 0.15, 0.10)
    k = min(len(scored), 4)
    total_fr = float(sum(fracs[:k]))
    end_time = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget))

    for i in range(k):
        if time.monotonic() > end_time - 2.0:
            break
        b = max(0.0, float(budget) * float(fracs[i]) / total_fr)
        if b < 2.0:
            continue
        a = scored[i][1]
        try:
            res_a, res_sc = run_optimizer(
                a, b, int(seed) + i * 131071, N,
                verbose=(verbose and i == 0),
                progress_start=0.0,
                eta_init=0.15
            )
        except Exception:
            res_a, res_sc = a, scored[i][0]
        if res_a is not None and len(res_a) == n and np.isfinite(res_sc):
            results.append((float(res_sc), res_a))


def clean_sequence_for_return(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return []
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    out = []
    for v in a.tolist():
        f = float(v)
        if not math.isfinite(f):
            f = 0.0
        if f < 0.0:
            f = 0.0
        if f > 1000.0:
            f = 1000.0
        out.append(f)
    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    total_budget = float(budget_s) if budget_s is not None else 1800.0
    if not math.isfinite(total_budget) or total_budget <= 0.0:
        total_budget = 1800.0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    hard_deadline = t0 + max(1.0, total_budget) - 3.0

    parent = load_parent_sequence()
    parent_len = parent.size if parent is not None else 0

    n_main = TARGET_LENGTH
    n_parent = None
    if parent is not None and 2048 <= parent_len < n_main:
        n_parent = int(parent_len)

    final_reserve = min(28.0, max(6.0, total_budget * 0.1))
    work_deadline = t0 + max(1.0, total_budget) - final_reserve - 3.0
    usable = max(0.0, work_deadline - time.monotonic())

    print(f"start: parent_len={parent_len} main_n={n_main} parent_n={n_parent} usable={usable:.1f}s")

    results = []

    if n_parent is not None and usable > 20.0:
        b_parent = 0.38 * usable
        b_main = 0.47 * usable
        if b_parent > 4.0:
            optimize_length(n_parent, parent, b_parent, int(seed) + 111, results, verbose=False)
            print(f"after parent len: best_internal="
                  f"{min(results, key=score_first)[0]:.6f}" if results else "no result")
        b_main = min(b_main, max(0.0, work_deadline - time.monotonic() - 2.0))
        if b_main > 4.0:
            optimize_length(n_main, parent, b_main, int(seed) + 222, results, verbose=True)
        print(f"after main len: best_internal="
              f"{min(results, key=score_first)[0]:.6f}" if results else "no result")
    else:
        b_main = 0.85 * usable
        if b_main > 4.0:
            optimize_length(n_main, parent, b_main, int(seed) + 222, results, verbose=True)
        print(f"after main len: best_internal="
              f"{min(results, key=score_first)[0]:.6f}" if results else "no result")

    if results:
        best_a = min(results, key=score_first)[1]
        remaining = max(0.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic() - final_reserve)
        if remaining > 10.0:
            n = len(best_a)
            N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
            try:
                pol_a, pol_sc = run_optimizer(
                    best_a, remaining, int(seed) + 999, N,
                    verbose=False, progress_start=0.65, eta_init=0.05
                )
                if pol_a is not None and len(pol_a) == n and np.isfinite(pol_sc):
                    results.append((float(pol_sc), pol_a))
            except Exception:
                pass

    if not results:
        if parent is not None and parent.size > 0:
            fallback = normalize_sequence(parent, float(min(parent.size, TARGET_LENGTH)))
        else:
            fallback = np.ones(TARGET_LENGTH, dtype=np.float64)
        return clean_sequence_for_return(fallback)

    results.sort(key=score_first)
    best_seq = results[0][1]
    best_value = float(results[0][0])

    evaluator = globals().get("evaluate_sequence", None)
    verify_limit = min(8, len(results))
    for i in range(verify_limit):
        if time.monotonic() > hard_deadline - 3.0:
            break
        a = results[i][1]
        seq = clean_sequence_for_return(a)
        if evaluator is not None:
            try:
                val = evaluator(seq)
            except Exception:
                val = None
            if val is not None and np.isfinite(val):
                if float(val) < best_value:
                    best_value = float(val)
                    best_seq = a
        elif np.isfinite(results[i][0]) and results[i][0] < best_value:
            best_value = float(results[i][0])
            best_seq = a

    print(f"final_internal/verified best={best_value:.6f} len={len(best_seq)}")
    return clean_sequence_for_return(best_seq)
```
